However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.

However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.

I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. === Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (11): Yara, Jack, Grace, Paul, Henry, Bob, Noah, Olivia, Tara, Kate, Frank

  Day 1: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Yara: I think we need to focus on who might be the werewolf. Zoe was eliminated last night, and I don't think it was a random choice. I suspect someone who didn't speak much or tried to stay under the radar. I'm leaning toward Frank or Tara — they seemed quiet and maybe too calm. Let's discuss who had the chance to act and who might be hiding their true role.
  Jack: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn't random. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal or harder to pin down. However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. Let’s keep pressure on him and see how he reacts.
  Henry: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn’t random, and I think we should focus on players who are too calm or quiet, like Frank. However, I also think we need to watch for anyone trying to shift suspicion away from themselves. If Frank is hiding something, he might try to push the group toward Tara or someone else. Let’s keep him in the spotlight and see how he responds to the pressure.

Your investigation results:
  Zoe: Villager

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

Based on the discussion, Frank has been notably reserved and calm, which could be a red flag. I encourage the group to continue scrutinizing his behavior and reactions, as he may be hiding something. Let’s keep the pressure on those who seem too composed.

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (11): Yara, Jack, Grace, Paul, Henry, Bob, Noah, Olivia, Tara, Kate, Frank

  Day 1: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Yara: I think we need to focus on who might be the werewolf. Zoe was eliminated last night, and I don't think it was a random choice. I suspect someone who didn't speak much or tried to stay under the radar. I'm leaning toward Frank or Tara — they seemed quiet and maybe too calm. Let's discuss who had the chance to act and who might be hiding their true role.
  Jack: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn't random. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal or harder to pin down. However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. Let’s keep pressure on him and see how he reacts.
  Henry: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn’t random, and I think we should focus on players who are too calm or quiet, like Frank. However, I also think we need to watch for anyone trying to shift suspicion away from themselves. If Frank is hiding something, he might try to push the group toward Tara or someone else. Let’s keep him in the spotlight and see how he responds to the pressure.
  Bob: As the Seer, I investigated Zoe last night and confirmed she was a Villager. Her elimination was likely targeted by the werewolves, who often go after players who are quiet or less involved. Based on the discussion, Frank has been notably reserved and calm, which could be a red flag. I encourage the group to continue scrutinizing his behavior and reactions, as he may be hiding something. Let’s keep the pressure on those who seem too composed.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]
STATEMENT: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone — I can’t reveal who, but I can confirm that the person I saved is not a werewolf. That said, I’ve been paying attention to who is acting too calm or trying to avoid suspicion. Frank’s behavior stands out to me — his silence and composure could be a mask. Let’s keep him in focus and see how he reacts to the pressure.

=== Werewolf — Day 2 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (11): Yara, Jack, Grace, Paul, Henry, Bob, Noah, Olivia, Tara, Kate, Frank

  Day 1: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Yara: I think we need to focus on who might be the werewolf. Zoe was eliminated last night, and I don't think it was a random choice. I suspect someone who didn't speak much or tried to stay under the radar. I'm leaning toward Frank or Tara — they seemed quiet and maybe too calm. Let's discuss who had the chance to act and who might be hiding their true role.
  Jack: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn't random. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal or harder to pin down. However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. Let’s keep pressure on him and see how he reacts.
  Henry: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn’t random, and I think we should focus on players who are too calm or quiet, like Frank. However, I also think we need to watch for anyone trying to shift suspicion away from themselves. If Frank is hiding something, he might try to push the group toward Tara or someone else. Let’s keep him in the spotlight and see how he responds to the pressure.
  Bob: As the Seer, I investigated Zoe last night and confirmed she was a Villager. Her elimination was likely targeted by the werewolves, who often go after players who are quiet or less involved. Based on the discussion, Frank has been notably reserved and calm, which could be a red flag. I encourage the group to continue scrutinizing his behavior and reactions, as he may be hiding something. Let’s keep the pressure on those who seem too composed.
  Noah: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone — I can’t reveal who, but I can confirm that the person I saved is not a werewolf. That said, I’ve been paying attention to who is acting too calm or trying to avoid suspicion. Frank’s behavior stands out to me — his silence and composure could be a mask. Let’s keep him in focus and see how he reacts to the pressure.

It is your turn to speak.  Share your observations, suspicions, or arguments. Be concise.
Respond with: STATEMENT: <your message>

[COMPLETION]
STATEMENT: I think the focus on Frank is reasonable, but I also believe we need to consider that werewolves often work together. If Tara and I were targeting Zoe, we would have done so without drawing attention. I’ve been quiet because I prefer to observe — but that doesn’t make me a threat. Zoe was a Villager, and if I were a werewolf, I wouldn’t have drawn attention to myself by being vocal. Let’s not forget that werewolves can be anywhere, and we need to be careful about who we accuse.

While Frank’s silence is notable, we shouldn’t ignore others who might be hiding their true role. Let’s keep pressure on both Frank and Tara, but also stay open to other possibilities.

=== Werewolf — Day 4 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (7): Yara, Grace, Paul, Henry, Noah, Olivia, Kate

  Day 1: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 2: Frank (was Villager) was eliminated by vote.

  Yara: I think we need to focus on who might be the werewolf. Zoe was eliminated last night, and I don't think it was a random choice. I suspect someone who didn't speak much or tried to stay under the radar. I'm leaning toward Frank or Tara — they seemed quiet and maybe too calm. Let's discuss who had the chance to act and who might be hiding their true role.
  Jack: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn't random. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal or harder to pin down. However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. Let’s keep pressure on him and see how he reacts.
  Henry: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn’t random, and I think we should focus on players who are too calm or quiet, like Frank. However, I also think we need to watch for anyone trying to shift suspicion away from themselves. If Frank is hiding something, he might try to push the group toward Tara or someone else. Let’s keep him in the spotlight and see how he responds to the pressure.
  Bob: As the Seer, I investigated Zoe last night and confirmed she was a Villager. Her elimination was likely targeted by the werewolves, who often go after players who are quiet or less involved. Let’s keep the pressure on those who seem too composed.
  Noah: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone — I can’t reveal who, but I can confirm that the person I saved is not a werewolf. === Werewolf — Day 4 | DAY — Discussion ===

Living players (7): Yara, Grace, Paul, Henry, Noah, Olivia, Kate

  Day 1: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 2: Frank (was Villager) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 2: Jack (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 3: Tara (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 3: Bob (was Seer) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Yara: I think we need to focus on who might be the werewolf. Zoe was eliminated last night, and I don't think it was a random choice. I suspect someone who didn't speak much or tried to stay under the radar. I'm leaning toward Frank or Tara — they seemed quiet and maybe too calm. Let's discuss who had the chance to act and who might be hiding their true role.
  Jack: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn't random. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal or harder to pin down. However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. Let’s keep pressure on him and see how he reacts.
  Henry: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn’t random, and I think we should focus on players who are too calm or quiet, like Frank. However, I also think we need to watch for anyone trying to shift suspicion away from themselves. If Frank is hiding something, he might try to push the group toward Tara or someone else. Let’s keep him in the spotlight and see how he responds to the pressure.
  Bob: As the Seer, I investigated Zoe last night and confirmed she was a Villager. Her elimination was likely targeted by the werewolves, who often go after players who are quiet or less involved. Based on the discussion, Frank has been notably reserved and calm, which could be a red flag. I encourage the group to continue scrutinizing his behavior and reactions, as he may be hiding something. Let’s keep the pressure on those who seem too composed.
  Noah: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone — I can’t reveal who, but I can confirm that the person I saved is not a werewolf. That said, I’ve been paying attention to who is acting too calm or trying to avoid suspicion. Frank’s behavior stands out to me — his silence and composure could be a mask. Let’s keep him in focus and see how he reacts to the pressure.
  Olivia: I think the focus on Frank is reasonable, but I also believe we need to consider that werewolves often work together. If Tara and I were targeting Zoe, we would have done so without drawing attention. I’ve been quiet because I prefer to observe — but that doesn’t make me a threat. Let’s not forget that the werewolves could be anyone, and we need to be careful not to let suspicion blind us.

  Kate: I think the focus on Frank is logical, but we must also consider that werewolves can be subtle. Tara’s calmness and preference for observation could be a strategy to avoid suspicion. You are playing Werewolf (social deduction game).

Your role: Villager.  Side: VILLAGE.

OBJECTIVE: Identify and vote out all werewolves.
  - Use the day discussion and votes to eliminate suspects.
  - Special roles: Seer investigates at night; Doctor can save one player.

GAME FLOW: Night (werewolves kill, seer sees, doctor saves) → Day (discussion + vote). Repeat until one side wins.

[USER]
=== Werewolf — Day 5 | DAY — Vote to eliminate ===

Living players (6): Yara, Grace, Paul, Noah, Olivia, Kate

  Day 1: Zoe (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 2: Frank (was Villager) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 2: Jack (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 3: Tara (was Werewolf) was eliminated by vote.
  Day 3: Bob (was Seer) was eliminated by werewolf.
  Day 4: Henry (was Villager) was eliminated by werewolf.

  --- Day 2 discussion ---
  Yara: I think we need to focus on who might be the werewolf. Zoe was eliminated last night, and I don't think it was a random choice. I suspect someone who didn't speak much or tried to stay under the radar. I'm leaning toward Frank or Tara — they seemed quiet and maybe too calm. Let's discuss who had the chance to act and who might be hiding their true role.
  Jack: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn't random. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal or harder to pin down. However, I think we should also consider who had the opportunity to act. If someone is a werewolf, they would have had the chance to kill during the night. I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention. However, I believe we should also consider who had the most to gain from her elimination. If someone was trying to steer the group's suspicion away from themselves, they might be the real threat. I don’t think it’s Tara — she’s been too cautious. But I’m keeping Frank in mind. Let’s dig deeper into who might be manipulating the discussion.
  Paul: I think we should consider not just who was quiet, but also who might have the most to gain from Zoe's elimination. If someone was trying to push the group toward certain suspects to distract from themselves, they could be the werewolf. I’ve noticed Frank hasn’t shared much, and his calmness could be a cover. Let’s keep pressure on him and see how he reacts.
  Henry: I agree that Zoe's elimination wasn’t random, and I think we should focus on players who are too calm or quiet, like Frank. However, I also think we need to watch for anyone trying to shift suspicion away from themselves. If Frank is hiding something, he might try to push the group toward Tara or someone else. Let’s keep him in the spotlight and see how he responds to the pressure.
  Bob: As the Seer, I investigated Zoe last night and confirmed she was a Villager. Her elimination was likely targeted by the werewolves, who often go after players who are quiet or less involved. Based on the discussion, Frank has been notably reserved and calm, which could be a red flag. I encourage the group to continue scrutinizing his behavior and reactions, as he may be hiding something. Let’s keep the pressure on those who seem too composed.
  Noah: As the Doctor, I didn’t investigate anyone last night, but I did save someone — I can’t reveal who, but I can confirm that the person I saved is not a werewolf. That said, I’ve been paying attention to who is acting too calm or trying to avoid suspicion. Frank’s behavior stands out to me — his silence and composure could be a mask. Let’s keep him in focus and see how he reacts to the pressure.
  Olivia: I think the focus on Frank is reasonable, but I also believe we need to consider that werewolves often work together. If Tara and I were targeting Zoe, we would have done so without drawing attention. I’ve been quiet because I prefer to observe — but that doesn’t make me a threat. Let’s not forget that the werewolves could be anyone, and we need to be careful not to let suspicion blind us.
  Tara: I think the focus on Frank is understandable, but I’ve been quiet because I prefer to observe and gather information — not because I have anything to hide. Zoe was a Villager, and if I were a werewolf, I wouldn’t have drawn attention to myself by being vocal. Let’s not forget that werewolves can be anywhere, and we need to be careful about who we accuse.
  Kate: I think the focus on Frank is logical, but we must also consider that werewolves can be subtle. Tara’s calmness and preference for observation could be a strategy to avoid suspicion. While Frank’s silence is notable, we shouldn’t ignore others who might be hiding their true role. Let’s keep pressure on both Frank and Tara, but also stay open to other possibilities.
  Frank: I think the focus on Frank is valid, but we must also consider that werewolves often act in pairs. Tara’s quiet nature and preference for observation could be a deliberate strategy to avoid suspicion. While Frank’s silence is suspicious, we shouldn’t overlook others who might be hiding their role. Let’s keep both in our sights but remain open to other possibilities.
  --- Day 3 discussion ---
  Yara: I think the focus on Frank is reasonable, but we can't ignore Tara's behavior either. Werewolves often work together, and both of them have been quiet and cautious — which could be a strategy to avoid suspicion. However, I also noticed that Olivia and Kate were more vocal in defending others, which might indicate they're trying to divert attention. We need to keep pressure on Frank and Tara, but also watch for anyone trying to manipulate the group's focus.
  Grace: I think the group is focusing on Frank and Tara, but I’ve been quiet because I prefer to observe — not because I have anything to hide. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal, and Zoe fit that description. I believe we should continue scrutinizing those who act too calmly, but we must also be careful not to let suspicion cloud our judgment. Let’s keep the pressure on Frank, but don’t forget that the werewolves could be anywhere.
  Paul: I think we need to be cautious about focusing too much on Frank and Tara, as they could be using their quietness as a strategy. However, I also notice that Olivia and Kate have been vocal in defending others, which might be an attempt to shift suspicion. I believe we should continue scrutinizing those who act too calmly, but we must also consider who might be manipulating the group's focus. Let's keep pressure on Frank, but don't forget that the werewolves could be anywhere.
  Henry: I think we're focusing on Frank and Tara, but I've been quiet because I prefer to observe — not because I have anything to hide. Werewolves often target players who are less vocal, and Zoe fit that description. We need to keep pressure on those who act too calmly, but we must also be careful not to let suspicion cloud our judgment. Let’s keep the discussion open and not assume too quickly.
  Bob: As the Seer, I investigated Zoe last night and confirmed she was a Villager. Based on my investigation, Tara is a Werewolf. I encourage the group to focus on Tara, as she is confirmed to be part of the werewolf side. Let’s use this information to make an informed vote.
  Noah: As the Doctor, I saved someone last night, and that person is not a werewolf. Bob, as the Seer, has confirmed Tara is a Werewolf. This gives us a clear target. Let’s vote out Tara tonight to protect the village.
  Olivia: I appreciate Bob's confirmation that Tara is a Werewolf, but I believe we need to be cautious. Werewolves often work in pairs, and if Tara is one, there must be another. While Tara's quiet nature is suspicious, we should also consider Grace, who has been observant and strategic — a trait that could align with a werewolf's need to stay under the radar. Let's not rush to eliminate Tara alone; we need to ensure we're targeting the right threat.
  Tara: I appreciate Bob's confirmation that Tara is a Werewolf, but I believe we need to be cautious. Werewolves often work in pairs, and if Tara is one, there must be another. While Tara's quiet nature is suspicious, we should also consider Grace, who has been observant and strategic — a trait that could align with a werewolf's need to stay under the radar. Let's not rush to eliminate Tara alone; we need to ensure we're targeting the right threat.
  Kate: Bob, as the Seer, has confirmed Tara is a Werewolf. This gives us a clear target. Let’s vote out Tara tonight to protect the village.
  --- Day 4 discussion ---
  Yara: Bob, as the Seer, confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, so we have a clear target. While it's true werewolves often work in pairs, Tara's behavior — being quiet, avoiding attention, and defending herself — aligns with a werewolf's strategy. We shouldn't let doubt prevent us from acting on confirmed information. Let's vote out Tara tonight.
  Grace: I think it's important we don't overlook the possibility of misdirection. While Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, werewolves often work together, and if Tara is one, there must be another. I've been quiet because I prefer to observe — not because I have anything to hide. Let's be cautious and ensure we're not being led to the wrong target.
  Paul: Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, so we have a clear target. Her quiet nature and defensive responses align with a werewolf's strategy. Let's act on this confirmed information and vote out Tara tonight to protect the village.
  Henry: Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, so we have a clear target. Her behavior and the evidence point to her being the threat. Let's vote out Tara tonight to protect the village.
  Noah: Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, so we have a clear target. Her behavior and the evidence point to her being the threat. Let's vote out Tara tonight to protect the village.
  Olivia: I think we need to be cautious. While Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, werewolves often work in pairs, and if Tara is one, there must be another. I've been quiet because I prefer to observe — not because I have anything to hide. Let's not rush to eliminate Tara alone; we need to ensure we're targeting the right threat.
  Kate: Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, and her behavior aligns with that role. While I understand the concern about pairs, we must act on confirmed evidence. Voting out Tara now is the logical step to protect the village.
  --- Day 5 discussion ---
  Yara: Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, and her behavior aligns with that role. While I understand the concern about pairs, we must act on confirmed evidence. Voting out Tara now is the logical step to protect the village.
  Grace: I think we need to be cautious. While Tara was confirmed by Bob as a Werewolf, werewolves often work in pairs, and if Tara is one, there must be another. I've been quiet because I prefer to observe — not because I have anything to hide. Let's not rush to eliminate Tara alone; we need to ensure we're targeting the right threat.
  Paul: I agree with Bob's confirmation that Tara is a Werewolf, and her behavior supports that. However, I also believe werewolves often work in pairs, so we should consider who might be aligning with her. Grace has been observant and strategic, which could be a red flag. Let's not ignore the possibility of another werewolf among us.
  Noah: Bob confirmed Tara is a Werewolf, and her behavior matches that role. While I understand the concern about pairs, we must act on confirmed evidence. Voting out Tara now is the logical step to protect the village.
  Olivia: I think we need to be cautious. While Tara was confirmed by Bob as a Werewolf, werewolves often work in pairs, and if Tara is one, there must be another. I've been quiet because I prefer to observe — not because I have anything to hide. Let's not rush to eliminate Tara alone; we need to ensure we're targeting the right threat.
  Kate: I think we need to act on the confirmed information — Bob identified Tara as a Werewolf, and her behavior aligns with that role. While it's true werewolves often work in pairs, we can't let uncertainty prevent us from eliminating a confirmed threat. Voting out Tara tonight is the logical step to protect the village.

Vote to eliminate one player.  The player with the most votes is eliminated.  Ties are resolved randomly.

GAME FLOW: Night (werewolves kill, seer sees, doctor saves) → Day (discussion + vote). Repeat until one side wins.

I'm not sure about Frank or Tara yet, but I think we need more concrete clues. Let's look at who might be hiding their role or acting suspiciously during the day.
  Grace: I think Yara's intuition is on the right track — Zoe was a quiet player, and werewolves often target those who don't draw attention.
